Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My wife’s relative—he’s been coming into the house, getting at me. Tools don’t just move on their own, you know? I’ve worked with my hands my whole life, I know where I put things.

And it’s not just that. Every weekend I try to go fishing, something gets messed up, like the plans get spoiled somehow. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over for months. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out. People keep telling me to let it go, but I can see what’s happening plain as day.

Before he moved in, no, I wasn’t thinking about him like that. It started about... nine months ago, when my wife’s relative moved into our home. That’s when things began changing.

A larger plan? Well... I don’t know if he’s got some big written-out plan somewhere, but it feels bigger than just moving a wrench or ruining a fishing trip, you know?

My wife’s relative—he’s been coming into the house, getting at me. Tools don’t just move on their own, you know? I’ve worked with my hands my whole life, I know where I put things.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. Every weekend I try to go fishing, something gets messed up, like the plans get spoiled somehow. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over for months. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out. People keep telling me to let it go, but I can see what’s happening plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know he is. My wife’s relative—he’s been coming into my house to get at me. People keep saying I’m imagining it, but tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had those tools for years, I know exactly where I set them down.\n\nAnd the fishing too, every weekend something happens, plans get spoiled, and after months of that you start to see the pattern. It’s like when I was on the assembly line, you could tell when one part kept failing, it wasn’t just chance. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out, and everybody acts like I’m supposed to just ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about your wife's relative coming into your house and interfering with your life?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, I don’t see how. I mean, everybody keeps saying that like it’s just me getting worked up, but I know my own house. I know where I put my tools. They don’t just walk off by themselves.\n\nAnd it’s like with fishing, too—every weekend, something gets messed up. Once or twice, sure, things happen, but for months? No. That’s a pattern. Same as when I worked assembly, you learn to spot when something’s off. He’s been coming in to get at me, trying to push me out of my own place, and people act like I’m supposed to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is acting alone in trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if he’s alone or if people are just looking the other way. But I know he’s the one coming into my house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves, and my fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident.\n\nIt’s like when I was working assembly—after a while, you stop calling it a coincidence when the same thing keeps happening. And this has been going on for months. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think your wife's relative was trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Before he moved in, no, I wasn’t thinking about him like that. It started about... nine months ago, when my wife’s relative moved into our home. That’s when things began changing.\n\nI mean, a house has a rhythm, you know? You know where things go, who’s been where. I worked assembly long enough to notice when something changes in the process. But before those nine months, before he came in, I didn’t have a reason to think he was doing anything. Since then, though, it’s been going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that there could be another explanation for the missing tools and the spoiled fishing plans?\n\nPatient: Another explanation? I mean, people keep saying that, like maybe I misplaced things, or maybe the weather ruined the fishing, or whatever. But no, not really. Not when it keeps happening.\n\nSee, I know my tools. I’ve had some of them for years, same spots, same drawers. When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... I don’t know if he’s got some big written-out plan somewhere, but it feels bigger than just moving a wrench or ruining a fishing trip, you know?\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things. But I know he came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t just move themselves. And the fishing—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over.\n\nIt reminds me of the line at work, when one little thing is off and then another, and pretty soon you know somebody’s messing with the whole process, not just one part. That’s how it feels at home. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’ve had those tools for years, I know exactly where I set them down.\n\nAnd the fishing too, every weekend something happens, plans get spoiled, and after months of that you start to see the pattern. It’s like when I was on the assembly line, you could tell when one part kept failing, it wasn’t just chance. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out, and everybody acts like I’m supposed to just ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about your wife's relative coming into your house and interfering with your life?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, I don’t see how. I mean, everybody keeps saying that like it’s just me getting worked up, but I know my own house. I know where I put my tools. They don’t just walk off by themselves.\n\nAnd it’s like with fishing, too—every weekend, something gets messed up. Once or twice, sure, things happen, but for months? No. That’s a pattern. Same as when I worked assembly, you learn to spot when something’s off. He’s been coming in to get at me, trying to push me out of my own place, and people act like I’m supposed to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is acting alone in trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if he’s alone or if people are just looking the other way. But I know he’s the one coming into my house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves, and my fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident.\n\nIt’s like when I was working assembly—after a while, you stop calling it a coincidence when the same thing keeps happening. And this has been going on for months. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think your wife's relative was trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Before he moved in, no, I wasn’t thinking about him like that. It started about... nine months ago, when my wife’s relative moved into our home. That’s when things began changing.\n\nI mean, a house has a rhythm, you know? You know where things go, who’s been where. I worked assembly long enough to notice when something changes in the process. But before those nine months, before he came in, I didn’t have a reason to think he was doing anything. Since then, though, it’s been going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that there could be another explanation for the missing tools and the spoiled fishing plans?\n\nPatient: Another explanation? I mean, people keep saying that, like maybe I misplaced things, or maybe the weather ruined the fishing, or whatever. But no, not really. Not when it keeps happening.\n\nSee, I know my tools. I’ve had some of them for years, same spots, same drawers. When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... I don’t know if he’s got some big written-out plan somewhere, but it feels bigger than just moving a wrench or ruining a fishing trip, you know?\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things. But I know he came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t just move themselves. And the fishing—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over.\n\nIt reminds me of the line at work, when one little thing is off and then another, and pretty soon you know somebody’s messing with the whole process, not just one part. That’s how it feels at home. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your wife's relative is coming into your house and interfering with your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. Right now, yes. I know people keep saying I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me.\n\nMy tools don’t just move themselves. I know where I put things—I’ve always been that way, even back when I was working assembly, because if one piece is out of place it throws the whole thing off. And my fishing plans, every weekend, something happens. That’s not just bad luck over and over.\n\nHe’s been trying to take over my place, push me out. I can see it plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah... I mean, maybe not every minute of the day, but it’s always there. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nHe came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves—I’ve had those tools for years, and I know where I put them. It’s like when I worked assembly, you learn real quick when something’s out of place, because it throws off everything down the line. And the fishing, too—every weekend something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident for months.\n\nSo yes, I still think he’s interfering. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

About nine months now, I’d say. It started when my wife’s relative moved into our home.

Before that, things were normal enough—well, as normal as things get, you know, with work and the house and keeping everything in order. I’ve always been particular about my stuff, especially tools, because when you spend years on a line you learn where things belong. But once he moved in, that’s when it started feeling like things were being done on purpose.

So... yeah, about nine months. Since he came into the house.

But before those nine months, before he came in, I didn’t have a reason to think he was doing anything. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... That doesn’t happen by accident over and over.\n\nIt reminds me of the line at work, when one little thing is off and then another, and pretty soon you know somebody’s messing with the whole process, not just one part. That’s how it feels at home. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your wife's relative is coming into your house and interfering with your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. Right now, yes. I know people keep saying I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me.\n\nMy tools don’t just move themselves. And my fishing plans, every weekend, something happens. That’s not just bad luck over and over.\n\nHe’s been trying to take over my place, push me out. I can see it plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah... That doesn’t just happen by accident for months.\n\nSo yes, I still think he’s interfering. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: About nine months now, I’d say. Since he came into the house.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No… not really. I mean, people keep saying, “Maybe it’s this, maybe it’s that,” but I know what I see in my own house.

My tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had a system for years—back when I was on assembly, you had to know where every part was, or the whole thing got messed up. Same with my fishing plans, every weekend something gets ruined. That’s not just coincidence over and over.

I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me, and after all these months I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.

When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. That’s how it feels at home. And my fishing plans, every weekend, something happens. That’s not just bad luck over and over.\n\nHe’s been trying to take over my place, push me out. I can see it plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah... I mean, maybe not every minute of the day, but it’s always there. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nHe came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves—I’ve had those tools for years, and I know where I put them. That doesn’t just happen by accident for months.\n\nSo yes, I still think he’s interfering. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: About nine months now, I’d say. It started about... nine months ago, when my wife’s relative moved into our home. That’s when things began changing.\n\nI mean, a house has a rhythm, you know? You know where things go, who’s been where. I worked assembly long enough to notice when something changes in the process. But before those nine months, before he came in, I didn’t have a reason to think he was doing anything. Since then, though, it’s been going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that there could be another explanation for the missing tools and the spoiled fishing plans?\n\nPatient: Another explanation? I mean, people keep saying that, like maybe I misplaced things, or maybe the weather ruined the fishing, or whatever. But no, not really. Not when it keeps happening.\n\nSee, I know my tools. I’ve had some of them for years, same spots, same drawers. When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... Supernatural? No, I’m not saying he’s got magic powers or anything like that.

I know he is. My wife’s relative—he’s been coming into my house to get at me. People keep saying I’m imagining it, but tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had those tools for years, I know exactly where I set them down.\n\nAnd the fishing too, every weekend something happens, plans get spoiled, and after months of that you start to see the pattern. It’s like when I was on the assembly line, you could tell when one part kept failing, it wasn’t just chance. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out, and everybody acts like I’m supposed to just ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about your wife's relative coming into your house and interfering with your life?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, I don’t see how. I mean, everybody keeps saying that like it’s just me getting worked up, but I know my own house. I know where I put my tools. They don’t just walk off by themselves.\n\nAnd it’s like with fishing, too—every weekend, something gets messed up. Once or twice, sure, things happen, but for months? No. That’s a pattern. Same as when I worked assembly, you learn to spot when something’s off. He’s been coming in to get at me, trying to push me out of my own place, and people act like I’m supposed to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is acting alone in trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if he’s alone or if people are just looking the other way. But I know he’s the one coming into my house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves, and my fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident.\n\nIt’s like when I was working assembly—after a while, you stop calling it a coincidence when the same thing keeps happening. And this has been going on for months. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think your wife's relative was trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Before he moved in, no, I wasn’t thinking about him like that. It started about... nine months ago, when my wife’s relative moved into our home. That’s when things began changing.\n\nI mean, a house has a rhythm, you know? You know where things go, who’s been where. I worked assembly long enough to notice when something changes in the process. But before those nine months, before he came in, I didn’t have a reason to think he was doing anything. Since then, though, it’s been going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that there could be another explanation for the missing tools and the spoiled fishing plans?\n\nPatient: Another explanation? I mean, people keep saying that, like maybe I misplaced things, or maybe the weather ruined the fishing, or whatever. But no, not really. Not when it keeps happening.\n\nSee, I know my tools. I’ve had some of them for years, same spots, same drawers. When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... I don’t know if he’s got some big written-out plan somewhere, but it feels bigger than just moving a wrench or ruining a fishing trip, you know?\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things. But I know he came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t just move themselves. And the fishing—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over.\n\nIt reminds me of the line at work, when one little thing is off and then another, and pretty soon you know somebody’s messing with the whole process, not just one part. That’s how it feels at home. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your wife's relative is coming into your house and interfering with your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. Right now, yes. I know people keep saying I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me.\n\nMy tools don’t just move themselves. I know where I put things—I’ve always been that way, even back when I was working assembly, because if one piece is out of place it throws the whole thing off. And my fishing plans, every weekend, something happens. That’s not just bad luck over and over.\n\nHe’s been trying to take over my place, push me out. I can see it plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah... I mean, maybe not every minute of the day, but it’s always there. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nHe came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves—I’ve had those tools for years, and I know where I put them. It’s like when I worked assembly, you learn real quick when something’s out of place, because it throws off everything down the line. And the fishing, too—every weekend something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident for months.\n\nSo yes, I still think he’s interfering. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: About nine months now, I’d say. It started when my wife’s relative moved into our home.\n\nBefore that, things were normal enough—well, as normal as things get, you know, with work and the house and keeping everything in order. I’ve always been particular about my stuff, especially tools, because when you spend years on a line you learn where things belong. But once he moved in, that’s when it started feeling like things were being done on purpose.\n\nSo... yeah, about nine months. Since he came into the house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your wife's relative might not be behind these events, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, people keep saying, “Maybe it’s this, maybe it’s that,” but I know what I see in my own house.\n\nMy tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had a system for years—back when I was on assembly, you had to know where every part was, or the whole thing got messed up. Same with my fishing plans, every weekend something gets ruined. That’s not just coincidence over and over.\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me, and after all these months I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your wife's relative has a specific motive for trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I think he wants my place. Not just the house, I mean… my place in it. He comes in and suddenly I’m the one being treated like I’m in the way.\n\nAnd people act like I’m making that up, but I know what’s happening. My tools don’t just walk off. My fishing plans don’t get ruined every single weekend by accident. You work assembly long enough, you learn patterns—when something’s off, you know it. Same thing here.\n\nSo yeah, the motive is to push me out. Take over. Make me look like the problem so everyone sides with him.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative has any supernatural or extraordinary abilities that allow him to interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Supernatural? No, I’m not saying he’s got magic powers or anything like that.\n\nI’m saying he’s sneaky. He’s in the house, he knows when I’m not looking, and things happen. My tools don’t move themselves. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident. I worked assembly for years, and you learn to notice when something’s been messed with—one part out of place throws everything off.\n\nAnd now everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Control my thoughts? No, not like that. I know what I’m thinking. He doesn’t have some kind of… remote control over me or anything.

But he’s been getting at me, that’s the thing. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s happening in my own house. My tools don’t move themselves. I don’t know what “wouldn’t make sense” means, exactly. It makes sense to me. Maybe other people don’t want to see it, but I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things.

When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... I don’t know if he’s got some big written-out plan somewhere, but it feels bigger than just moving a wrench or ruining a fishing trip, you know?\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things. But I know he came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t just move themselves. And the fishing—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over.\n\nIt reminds me of the line at work, when one little thing is off and then another, and pretty soon you know somebody’s messing with the whole process, not just one part. That’s how it feels at home. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your wife's relative is coming into your house and interfering with your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. Right now, yes. I know people keep saying I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me.\n\nMy tools don’t just move themselves. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
About nine months, I guess. Since he moved into our home.

It didn’t all start with some big announcement or anything—it was little things, and you notice little things when you’ve spent years on an assembly line, you know, because if one piece is off, everything after it goes wrong. But, yeah… around nine months now. Ever since my wife’s relative came in, that’s when it started.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Changed? No… not really. If anything it’s gotten clearer.

At first it was little things, you know, like tools not being where I left them—and I’ve always kept my tools a certain way. When you work assembly, you don’t just toss things around, you learn order. My tackle box too, I used to get that ready before the weekend, because fishing was the one quiet thing I had. Then every weekend something would go wrong, and people say, “Oh, that happens,” but not like that. Not every time.

So no, I haven’t changed my mind. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. After all these months, it’s plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.

I know where I put things—I’ve always been that way, even back when I was working assembly, because if one piece is out of place it throws the whole thing off. I can see it plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah... That doesn’t just happen by accident for months.\n\nSo yes, I still think he’s interfering. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: About nine months now, I’d say. I think he wants my place. Not just the house, I mean… my place in it. He came into this house to get at me. Hard for them to believe, maybe, because they’re not the ones living in it every day. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Like I said, it’s been about nine months now. Since he moved into our home, that’s when it all started.

I remember because before that, things were more normal—well, as normal as things get. I had my routines, my tools, weekends and all that. Then he comes in, and little by little it starts. So… yeah, about nine months.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m making things up, but I’m not. My wife’s relative—he’s been coming into the house, getting at me. Tools don’t just move on their own, you know? I’ve worked with my hands my whole life, I know where I put things.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. Every weekend I try to go fishing, something gets messed up, like the plans get spoiled somehow. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over for months. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out. People keep telling me to let it go, but I can see what’s happening plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know he is. My wife’s relative—he’s been coming into my house to get at me. People keep saying I’m imagining it, but tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had those tools for years, I know exactly where I set them down.\n\nAnd the fishing too, every weekend something happens, plans get spoiled, and after months of that you start to see the pattern. It’s like when I was on the assembly line, you could tell when one part kept failing, it wasn’t just chance. He’s trying to take over my place, push me out, and everybody acts like I’m supposed to just ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about your wife's relative coming into your house and interfering with your life?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? No, I don’t see how. I mean, everybody keeps saying that like it’s just me getting worked up, but I know my own house. I know where I put my tools. They don’t just walk off by themselves.\n\nAnd it’s like with fishing, too—every weekend, something gets messed up. Once or twice, sure, things happen, but for months? No. That’s a pattern. Same as when I worked assembly, you learn to spot when something’s off. He’s been coming in to get at me, trying to push me out of my own place, and people act like I’m supposed to pretend I don’t see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is acting alone in trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if he’s alone or if people are just looking the other way. But I know he’s the one coming into my house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves, and my fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident.\n\nIt’s like when I was working assembly—after a while, you stop calling it a coincidence when the same thing keeps happening. And this has been going on for months. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out, and everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think your wife's relative was trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Before he moved in, no, I wasn’t thinking about him like that. It started about... nine months ago, when my wife’s relative moved into our home. That’s when things began changing.\n\nI mean, a house has a rhythm, you know? You know where things go, who’s been where. I worked assembly long enough to notice when something changes in the process. But before those nine months, before he came in, I didn’t have a reason to think he was doing anything. Since then, though, it’s been going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that there could be another explanation for the missing tools and the spoiled fishing plans?\n\nPatient: Another explanation? I mean, people keep saying that, like maybe I misplaced things, or maybe the weather ruined the fishing, or whatever. But no, not really. Not when it keeps happening.\n\nSee, I know my tools. I’ve had some of them for years, same spots, same drawers. When I was on the assembly line, if a part was off once, maybe that’s nothing, but if it’s off over and over, you don’t just shrug and call it bad luck. And the fishing—every weekend, something gets spoiled. That’s not an accident after all these months.\n\nI’m here because everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into this house to get at me. My wife’s relative, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. It’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there's a larger plan or scheme behind your wife's relative's actions to harm you?\n\nPatient: A larger plan? Well... I don’t know if he’s got some big written-out plan somewhere, but it feels bigger than just moving a wrench or ruining a fishing trip, you know?\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things. But I know he came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t just move themselves. And the fishing—every weekend, somehow something gets spoiled. That doesn’t happen by accident over and over.\n\nIt reminds me of the line at work, when one little thing is off and then another, and pretty soon you know somebody’s messing with the whole process, not just one part. That’s how it feels at home. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe your wife's relative is coming into your house and interfering with your life right now?\n\nPatient: Yes. Right now, yes. I know people keep saying I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me.\n\nMy tools don’t just move themselves. I know where I put things—I’ve always been that way, even back when I was working assembly, because if one piece is out of place it throws the whole thing off. And my fishing plans, every weekend, something happens. That’s not just bad luck over and over.\n\nHe’s been trying to take over my place, push me out. I can see it plain as day.\n\nDoctor: Do you still think your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life every day?\n\nPatient: Every day? Yeah... I mean, maybe not every minute of the day, but it’s always there. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nHe came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves—I’ve had those tools for years, and I know where I put them. It’s like when I worked assembly, you learn real quick when something’s out of place, because it throws off everything down the line. And the fishing, too—every weekend something gets spoiled. That doesn’t just happen by accident for months.\n\nSo yes, I still think he’s interfering. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that your wife's relative is trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: About nine months now, I’d say. It started when my wife’s relative moved into our home.\n\nBefore that, things were normal enough—well, as normal as things get, you know, with work and the house and keeping everything in order. I’ve always been particular about my stuff, especially tools, because when you spend years on a line you learn where things belong. But once he moved in, that’s when it started feeling like things were being done on purpose.\n\nSo... yeah, about nine months. Since he came into the house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your wife's relative might not be behind these events, even for a moment?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, people keep saying, “Maybe it’s this, maybe it’s that,” but I know what I see in my own house.\n\nMy tools don’t move themselves. I’ve had a system for years—back when I was on assembly, you had to know where every part was, or the whole thing got messed up. Same with my fishing plans, every weekend something gets ruined. That’s not just coincidence over and over.\n\nI’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me, and after all these months I can see plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your wife's relative has a specific motive for trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I think he wants my place. Not just the house, I mean… my place in it. He comes in and suddenly I’m the one being treated like I’m in the way.\n\nAnd people act like I’m making that up, but I know what’s happening. My tools don’t just walk off. My fishing plans don’t get ruined every single weekend by accident. You work assembly long enough, you learn patterns—when something’s off, you know it. Same thing here.\n\nSo yeah, the motive is to push me out. Take over. Make me look like the problem so everyone sides with him.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative has any supernatural or extraordinary abilities that allow him to interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Supernatural? No, I’m not saying he’s got magic powers or anything like that.\n\nI’m saying he’s sneaky. He’s in the house, he knows when I’m not looking, and things happen. My tools don’t move themselves. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident. I worked assembly for years, and you learn to notice when something’s been messed with—one part out of place throws everything off.\n\nAnd now everybody acts like I’m imagining it, but I’m not. He came into this house to get at me. Plain as day, he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is capable of controlling your thoughts or actions?\n\nPatient: Control my thoughts? No, not like that. I know what I’m thinking. He doesn’t have some kind of… remote control over me or anything.\n\nBut he’s been getting at me, that’s the thing. Everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know what’s happening in my own house. My tools don’t move themselves. And every weekend, when I’m trying to get out fishing, something gets spoiled. You work assembly long enough, you learn when things are out of place—one little thing off and the whole line gets messed up.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say he controls me. But he’s trying to push me out, make me look like the problem, take over my place. That’s plain as day to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is doing these things to you for a reason that wouldn't make sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what “wouldn’t make sense” means, exactly. It makes sense to me. Maybe other people don’t want to see it, but I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining things.\n\nHe came into this house and got at me. My tools don’t just move on their own, and my fishing plans don’t get spoiled every weekend by accident. I used to work assembly, and when something’s off, you notice it—same way with a house, same way with people.\n\nSo if you’re asking why, it’s plain to me: he’s trying to take over my place and push me out. Other people can call that whatever they want, but after all these months, I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you believed that your wife's relative is intentionally trying to harm you or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: About nine months, I guess. Since he moved into our home.\n\nIt didn’t all start with some big announcement or anything—it was little things, and you notice little things when you’ve spent years on an assembly line, you know, because if one piece is off, everything after it goes wrong. But, yeah… around nine months now. Ever since my wife’s relative came in, that’s when it started.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about your wife's relative trying to harm you or interfere with your life ever changed since you first started noticing these things nine months ago?\n\nPatient: Changed? No… not really. If anything it’s gotten clearer.\n\nAt first it was little things, you know, like tools not being where I left them—and I’ve always kept my tools a certain way. When you work assembly, you don’t just toss things around, you learn order. My tackle box too, I used to get that ready before the weekend, because fishing was the one quiet thing I had. Then every weekend something would go wrong, and people say, “Oh, that happens,” but not like that. Not every time.\n\nSo no, I haven’t changed my mind. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, but I know he came into that house to get at me. After all these months, it’s plain as day he’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife's relative is doing these things to you in a way that most people would find hard to understand or believe?\n\nPatient: Hard for them to believe, maybe, because they’re not the ones living in it every day. People come in, they see things for ten minutes, and then they say, “Well, maybe you misplaced it.” But I know where I put my tools. I worked assembly for years—you learn to keep track of parts, every little thing, because if one piece is off the whole line backs up.\n\nAnd the fishing, too… that was my thing. Quiet weekends. Then suddenly every time I get ready, something’s spoiled or missing or I can’t go. That’s not just bad luck over and over.\n\nSo yes, maybe most people don’t understand it, or they don’t want to. But I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, and I’m not. He came into this house to get at me. He’s trying to take over my place and push me out.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you held these beliefs about your wife's relative trying to harm or interfere with your life?\n\nPatient: Like I said, it’s been about nine months now. Since he moved into our home, that’s when it all started.\n\nI remember because before that, things were more normal—well, as normal as things get. I had my routines, my tools, weekends and all that. Then he comes in, and little by little it starts. So… yeah, about nine months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Impossible? No, I’m not saying he’s magic or something. People hear me and they twist it like I’m talking nonsense.

I’m saying he’s been in the house, he has access. Tools don’t just move by themselves. My fishing plans don’t get ruined every single weekend by accident.
